# Changelog

All notable changes to the Classroom Token Hub project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project follows semantic versioning principles.


## [Unreleased]

### Security

- **Admin login accepted an attacker-supplied redirect target (2026-09-12)** — `admin.login` read `?next=` and passed it straight to `redirect()` with no validation at either of its two success branches. Any absolute URL was honoured, so a genuine CTH login flow could terminate on an attacker-controlled host. To be precise about the impact: an open redirect does not itself disclose the passphrase or TOTP code to the destination — the credentials are submitted to CTH and never reach the attacker's server. The harm is that a trusted login flow ends somewhere untrusted, which lends CTH's origin to convincing phishing, credential re-prompting, and malicious follow-on flows. This was a live open redirect, not a scanner artifact.

  The shared `is_safe_url` helper had a second, independent defect: it did not normalize backslashes before parsing. Browsers resolve `/\evil.example` and `\/evil.example` as protocol-relative URLs to `evil.example`, while `urlparse` reads them as local paths, so all three backslash forms returned `True`. Every caller of the helper inherited the bypass — student, sysadmin and `app/auth.py` redirect paths included — so the fix went into the helper rather than into the two routes a scanner happened to flag.

  `safe_redirect_target(target, fallback)` in `app/utils/helpers.py` is now the single way a `?next=` value becomes a redirect. It normalizes backslashes, then *rejects* rather than sanitizes anything bearing a scheme, a netloc, a protocol-relative prefix, or a non-root-relative path, returning the trusted fallback instead; in-app paths keep their query string and fragment. `admin.login` and `main.switch_view` now route through it, and `is_safe_url` normalizes backslashes for its remaining callers.

  Covered by `tests/test_open_redirect_hardening.py` (22 cases). The pre-fix behaviour was reproduced directly first: the old helper returned `True` for all three backslash forms, and the old admin branch returned `https://evil.example/phish` verbatim.

- **Roster sync returned raw exception text to the browser (2026-09-12)** — The per-row `except` in the roster sync endpoint appended `str(e)` to an `errors` list that is serialized into the JSON response. Row failures there are mostly `SQLAlchemy` errors, whose text carries schema detail and the bound parameters — which on this endpoint are student first and last names, i.e. exactly the PII `INV-ARC-005` keeps out of unencrypted surfaces. The handler now logs via `logger.exception` (keeping the traceback server-side, where it is useful) and returns `"Row N: Could not be processed."`. The row number, which is the only part a teacher can act on, is preserved.

- **A debit could authorize against a balance that silently omitted another debit (2026-09-11, #1364)** — Debit FEATs serialized on the `seats` row, but settlement never took that lock, so a debit and a settlement for the same seat did not serialize at all. `get_available_balance` was also two statements, posted then pending, and a settlement committing between them moved a pending debit out of the pending term after the posted term had already been read. The debit was counted in neither term, the sufficiency check passed, and the seat could overdraw. Because posted rows are immutable (`INV-LED-002`), that overdraft could not be corrected by rewriting history.

  Settlement now locks the seat row before the class row. `pay_rent` and `execute_purchase_insurance`, which checked affordability without any lock, now take the seat lock before reading the balance and hold it through the debit. `get_available_balance` and `get_available_balances` evaluate posted and pending as scalar subqueries of one statement, so the two terms always come from the same database snapshot. `INV-LED-015` in `DOM-LED-001` now states which row serializes a seat's money and in what order the locks are taken.

  Lock-order audit: transfer, store purchase and direct grant already take the seat lock first. No path takes the class row before the seat. `insurance_claim_feat` locks only `EntitlementEvent` rows and reads no balance. The only live caller of ledger resolution is store purchase, which already holds the seat lock. `apply_overdraft_fee_if_needed` has no callers.

  Covered by `tests/dom/ledger/test_seat_balance_serialization.py`. It holds the seat lock on a second connection and shows settlement cannot proceed, with the snapshot rows created first so the foreign key's `FOR KEY SHARE` lock cannot make it pass by accident. It also asserts the lock order, that rent and insurance payments lock before reading, and that an available-balance read is a single statement. Each test was watched failing with its fix removed.

  Taking the lock also meant replay resolution had to move under it. A same-key retry that waits on the seat lock acts on whatever it read before waiting. `execute_purchase_insurance` looked up "this command already granted" before the lock, so a retry that waited out its own first attempt fell through to the coverage check and reported `POLICY_ALREADY_HELD` for its own purchase. It now takes the seat lock first. `pay_rent` had a worse replay defect, and one that occurred even with no concurrency. A same-key partial-payment replay counted its own committed debit in `paid_before`, got the same ledger row back from the idempotent write, and reported the payment a second time with a remaining balance that was never paid. For example, replaying a 20.00 payment on a 50.00 bill reported 10.00 remaining instead of 30.00. The replay now resolves from the command's own ledger row, under the lock, and reports the original payment. Three more tests cover this: both replay lookups run after the seat lock, and a partial rent replay reports the original amount and writes nothing. Each was watched failing with its fix removed.

- **Student PII no longer reaches the application log (2026-09-09)** — The failure path in `admin.delete_student` logged `identity_profile.full_name`, putting a decrypted student name into an unencrypted, off-host-shipped log in violation of `INV-ARC-005`. It now logs `seat_id` and uses `logger.exception`, so the traceback is kept and the student is not. The flash messages still name the student: those are shown to the authenticated teacher who owns the class, which is authorized display, not logging.

- **CSRF token no longer reaches the application log (2026-09-09)** — The same route logged `dict(request.form)` wholesale on entry, which included the session-bound `csrf_token`. It now records the sorted field *names* only, keeping the diagnostic without the secret. Both leaks are covered by `tests/dom/identity/test_student_deletion_log_hygiene.py`, which asserts against real emitted log records and fails if either value returns.

- **Removed a developer's local filesystem path from the public repository (2026-09-09)** — 129 occurrences of an absolute `/Users/<name>/...` path across five documents in `docs/` disclosed a maintainer's real name and local directory layout. Paths are now repository-relative, which also repairs the broken absolute markdown links in the archived route audits.

### Removed

- **`bonus_rate` deleted from payroll settings (2026-09-10)** — A v1 artifact that never became a feature: a Float column on `PayrollSettings`, a form field labelled "Bonus Rate ($ per minute)" on `PayrollSettingsForm`, and membership in `_FROZEN_POLICY_FIELDS`. Nothing else. No template rendered it, no route, service or FEAT read it, no test asserted it, and no document under `docs/INVARIANT/`, `docs/DOMAIN/`, `docs/FEATURE-EXECUTION/`, `docs/SPEC/` or `docs/STANDARD_OPERATING_PROCEDURES/` has ever required it. A teacher could type a number into it and the number went nowhere.

  That is worse than an unfinished feature. `payroll_settings` is an append-only versioned policy table (`DOM-POL-001` §VI.1), so every row is a contract the teacher is told they submitted — and a frozen field that silently means nothing puts a fictional term inside an immutable contract.

  Dropping a column from an immutable-policy table destroys what historical rows asserted, so this was checked against real data first: every existing row held `0.0`, the column default, so no policy version loses a term it actually carried. Migration `a4f2b7c91d38`; upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade verified. Because `_SUBMITTABLE_FIELDS` in `payroll_settings_service` is derived from `_FROZEN_POLICY_FIELDS`, a caller still passing `bonus_rate` now raises rather than silently writing a column that no longer exists.

- **Prohibited version pointer dropped from `payroll_settings` (2026-09-10)** — `payroll_settings.policy_version_id`, its index and its foreign key into `policy_versions` are an alternative version pointer on a Policies repository table. `DOM-POL-001` §VI.0 prohibits that construct by name — "whether a self-referential FK on a Policies table or an external version-tracking table" — and says explicitly that `policy_versions` / `policy_transitions` record economic-policy evolution and are not a domain-policy versioning mechanism.

  The column arrived 2026-07-19 (`c3d4e5f8a9b`), when payroll settings still tracked lineage by pointing at `policy_versions`. The 2026-09-04 append-only conversion (`3bb29ef4e874`) made `policy_uuid` the version and dropped `is_active` for exactly this reason — its own docstring calls two current-policy projections "exactly the alternative version pointer DOM-POL-001 §VI.0 prohibits" — but walked past the second pointer. `rent_settings` and `hall_pass_settings`, converted in the same migration, carry no such column; `payroll_settings` was the outlier.

  Nothing could break: `PayrollSettings` never declared the column, so no route, service, FEAT, test or template ever read or wrote it, and all six rows held `NULL`. Migration `b7d3e2064c15`; upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade verified, FK discovered by column rather than by hardcoded name. `payroll_event.policy_version_id` is deliberately untouched — `PayrollEvent` is an operational fact in Productivity, not a Policies row, and freezing lineage on a historical fact is the contract (`FEAT-PROD-003` fails closed without it).

### Added

- **`SPEC-DES-001` supersedes the archived `FEAT-DES-001` as the design authority (2026-09-11)** — The design system's only written description was `FEAT-DES-001` v1.1, archived and marked non-normative, and it had drifted: it documents the teacher accent as `#d3af37` and the student accent as bronze `#ac8255`, while the implementation converged all three roles on `#D4A857`. A design system with no live normative document cannot be conformed to, and three separate defects below existed precisely because no rule forbade them.

  The new spec records the canonical brand values, requires the scales v1 omitted (spacing, type, motion, opacity, icon sizing — v1 tokenized only color and radius, against 25 distinct font sizes and 19 spacing values hardcoded in `style.css`), states the Bootstrap `--bs-*-rgb` parity rule, distinguishes a legitimate Jinja-computed inline style from a static violation, binds contrast obligations to `INV-ARC-020`, and adds machine-checkable conformance criteria. It is filed Normative Tier 2 and cites the archived predecessor inline rather than in Dependencies, per `SOP-DOC-000`.

### Changed

- **README brought back in line with the code (2026-09-11)** — Every claim in `README.md` was checked against the repository. Removed claims nothing implements: OpenTelemetry instrumentation (the packages are pinned but nothing imports them), post-claim PII deletion (a v1 behaviour tied to removed models), sysadmin user provisioning and 2FA resets from the web portal (sysadmins are CLI-created only), and sysadmin announcements (announcements are teacher-side). Corrected the rest: sign-up is three steps, not two; pay rates are per minute, not hourly; seat claim matches a name against the roster rather than a teacher-issued credential; the retired Analytics module is now the read-only Interpretation report; the migration head is no longer a merge point; CI runs Python 3.10, 3.11 and 3.13 against PostgreSQL 15 and 16. Added the shipped features the README never named — insurance, banking and savings interest, the Economic Engine, ledger settlement and the scheduled jobs, passkeys, issues, announcements, feature settings, in-app docs, maintenance mode — plus the optional environment variables and the design-token checker. `CSRF_SECRET_KEY` moved out of the required setup, since the app falls back to `SECRET_KEY` without it.

- **Every template now conforms to the `SPEC-DES-001` template contract, and a gate keeps it that way (2026-09-11)** — An audit of all 103 templates under `templates/` found 1,156 violations in 72 of them: 146 hardcoded colours, 471 static inline `style` attributes, 496 literal design values in page `<style>` blocks, and 43 page selectors that `style.css` also defined. All are resolved. `docs-site/` is a separately published artifact (§XIII) and was not in scope; the in-app docs under `templates/docs/` were.

  The spec already said this was mechanically checkable and should be gated; nothing did. `scripts/lint_design_tokens.py` now checks §XIV rules 1, 5, 6 and 7, and `tests/test_design_token_contract.py` runs it with one test per rule, plus probes proving each rule still detects its violation and still admits tokens, structural geometry and id selectors — so the gate cannot pass by going blind. Run the script directly on templates you are editing.

  One way it could have gone blind anyway: the scanner delimited blocks on a literal `</style>` / `</script>`, but HTML permits whitespace and ignored attributes inside an end tag, so a block closed with `</style >` was invisible to it — and a `<style>` block that never matched is a block whose every violation goes unreported. Flagged by CodeQL as `py/bad-tag-filter`. Both patterns now accept the full end-tag form while still rejecting near-misses like `</styled>`, with probes for the whitespace variants; each probe was confirmed to go undetected under the previous pattern.

  Shared layer. The six error pages carried near-identical blocks the spec had listed as outstanding; their chrome is now one `body.error-page` section in `style.css`. New token utilities (`.type-*`, `.tracking-*`, `.alpha-*`, `.text-accent`, `.scroll-panel`, `.icon-2xs`, `.icon-3xl`) give templates one class per token instead of an inline style. New tokens: `--icon-2xs`, `--icon-3xl`, and display sizes `--text-5xl` / `--text-6xl`, so a status code no longer borrows an icon token for text. `SPEC-DES-001` is v2.1.

  Behaviour. Static `style="display:none"` became the `hidden` attribute across 15 templates, `static/js/attendance.js` and `static/js/item-form-economy.js`, with every script that toggled those elements moved to `el.hidden` — Bootstrap's `[hidden]` rule is `!important`, so a leftover `style.display = 'block'` would have silently stopped showing the element.

  Defects found on the way:
  - Four contrast failures: the select-class panel heading was dark text on the dark green panel; the maintenance page's "bug" badge was white on the gold fill (~2.1:1); the getting-started Skip button hover was white on gold (2.2:1); timeline tag text sat at ~4.4:1.
  - `student_detail.html` put its styles in `{% block extra_styles %}`, which `layout_admin.html` does not define, so they never rendered. It now uses `extra_head`.
  - `static/sw.js` precached `style.css` but not `tokens.css`, so the offline page lost every token exactly when the network was down. Cache version bumped to v9.
  - Dead page CSS removed where it matched nothing or always lost to a shared `!important` rule.

  Deliberate visual changes worth reviewing. With no matching token, some hues now reuse the nearest semantic one: in the docs, IMPORTANT and TIP callouts share the `--primary` family; the timeline's Security (orange) and Philosophy (purple) categories now render gold-brown and graphite; the student troubleshooting card header is the role primary rather than brown. The six error pages share one card treatment, so 404 and 500 lose their rounded corners and the others get `--shadow-lg`. Several floating shadows are softer because `--shadow-lg` is lighter than the hand-written ones it replaces. Other values moved at most one step to the nearest token.

- **Auth form chrome is defined once instead of fourteen times, and the dead copies are gone (2026-09-11)** — The fourteen auth templates carried 58 distinct selectors between them in page-scoped `<style>` blocks, 21 of them duplicated across pages with visible drift. The label, subtitle, help-text, code-input and footer-link rules are now defined once in `style.css` under `.auth-body`; seven templates carry no `<style>` block at all, and the ones that remain hold only genuinely page-specific chrome (QR panels, step indicators, the strength meter, the sysadmin security notice).

  Most of what was deleted had never rendered. Every page copy of `.btn-primary`, `.btn-outline-primary`, `.btn-outline-secondary`, `.text-primary` and the bare `a` / `a:hover` rules was already beaten by `style.css`, which claims those selectors with `!important` — and `!important` wins over any specificity. Three of the dead `.btn-primary:hover` copies had independently drifted to a fill that would have failed contrast had it ever applied, which is precisely the risk of an invisible rule: it is edited, reviewed and merged as if it were live. Confirmed rule by rule in-browser before removal, and `.btn-outline-danger` was kept because `style.css` does not claim it.

  One visible change: `.subtitle` was gold on some pages and neutral on others. It is now `--text-secondary` everywhere. Gold at body size on white is 2.12:1 and was never legible as prose — `--accent-text-on-light` exists for accent text and is what the uppercase field labels use. `templates/admin_recovery_saved.html` and `admin_recovery_status.html`, which sat outside the redesign, were tokenized in the same pass; both were still on literal `background: white`, `1.75rem`, `3rem` and `letter-spacing: 0.3em`, and both carried the same dead gradient buttons.

  Re-validated per `INV-ARC-020` §X: a per-element contrast sweep across all six reachable auth routes — every rendered text node measured against its effective background, at the AA threshold appropriate to its computed size and weight — reports zero failures in all three role shells. `pytest tests/test_accessibility.py tests/test_axe_compliance.py tests/test_layout_accessibility_contract.py` passes (97 passed, 14 skipped), and all 103 templates parse.

- **`SPEC-DES-001` now distinguishes a design value from a structural one (2026-09-11)** — §IX permitted a page-scoped `<style>` block only if every value in it resolved to a token, which no template could satisfy. `min-height: 100dvh`, `width: 0%` on a meter that script drives, `border-radius: 50%` on a circular step indicator, `grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr auto` and a `max-width` capping a QR image are facts about one element's geometry, not decisions the design system owns; tokenizing them would mint tokens with a single call site each and make the scales meaningless.

  The rule now applies to design values — anything a token family could change system-wide — and admits structural literals with a falsifiable test: a value is structural only if changing the corresponding token everywhere would not be expected to change it, and when in doubt it is a design value. §XII conformance and §XIV enforcement were amended in the same edit so the criteria cannot be satisfied by a rule the criteria no longer match. §XIV also gains a rule against a selector appearing in both a page block and `style.css`, which is the defect the deletion above found and which produces no visible symptom.

- **The auth pages stack the brand above the form instead of setting it beside them (2026-09-11)** — Fourteen pages — three sign-in, the sign-up and credential flows, and the student recovery layout — each carried their own copy of a two-column split: form on the left, a `linear-gradient` panel on the right holding a 400×400px PNG logo. The panel was decorative only, and every copy ended in the same `@media (max-width: 768px) { .login-right { display: none } }`, so the brand vanished entirely on a phone — which is where much of the audience for a student sign-in page actually is.

  They now share one layout defined once in `style.css`: a full-width brand band on top (`.auth-brand`), the interactive panel beneath (`.auth-body`). The band is filled with `var(--primary)`, so it takes Advisor Green, Steward Blue, or Guardian Gray from the role class already on `<body>` — no per-page override and no role conditional. The brand inside it is the text wordmark in the same vertical stack the public landing hero uses, rendered from a new `macros/auth_brand.html` rather than transcribed fourteen times.

  The card width is pinned rather than inherited. These pages center the card as a flex item, so the previous `width: 100%` resolved against a shrink-to-fit parent and every page ended up as wide as its own longest line of copy — the teacher card measured 346px and the student card 457px on the same viewport, because the student subtitle is a longer sentence. All fourteen are now 400px. The narrow-screen cap is measured against the viewport, not the parent: `max-width: 100%` cannot rescue a flex item whose automatic minimum size is pinned by its specified width, so the card would have overflowed instead of shrinking. Checked down to a 320px viewport with no horizontal overflow.

  The three PNG logos are gone from the templates. An image logo cannot take a role theme, cannot be recolored by a token, and needs a redundant `alt` string; the wordmark answers all three and stays legible at every width. Net template change is −961 lines against +370, nearly all of it deleted duplication.

  Contrast validated per `INV-ARC-020` §X on all three bands: the white wordmark on `--primary` is 9.56:1 (teacher), 8.25:1 (student) and 13.20:1 (sysadmin), all clearing AA for normal text. The gold leading icons are decorative `aria-hidden` and clear the 3:1 large-text threshold at 4.34 / 3.75 / 5.99:1. Verified in-browser across all three role shells at desktop and 375px widths with no console errors. Recorded in `SPEC-DES-001` §IX under "The brand mark" and "The auth shell".

- **Design tokens now cover the scales the stylesheet was hardcoding (2026-09-11)** — Added spacing, type, weight, line-height, motion, opacity, icon-size, layout and breakpoint scales to `tokens.css`, plus `.icon-*` utilities so icon sizing stops being an inline `font-size`. Sysadmin aliases (`--sysadmin-primary`, `--sysadmin-primary-dark`, `--sysadmin-accent`, `--sysadmin-bottom-nav-height`) now alias the theme tokens instead of restating their literals, so they follow the theme when it moves rather than silently diverging from it.

  Remaining hardcoded colors were given token homes and replaced: the student shell's nav-active fill and brand hover accents, the alert contrast overrides, the sysadmin gradient and sign-out hover, and every bare `#fff`/`#5f5f5c`. `#000` inside `color-mix()` is left alone — it is a mixing endpoint, not a color choice. `templates/admin_dashboard.html` lost all seven inline `color:` styles in favor of `text-muted`.

### Fixed

- **Six `var()` references pointed at tokens that do not exist (2026-09-11)** — `--student-primary`, `--shadow`, `--text-light`, `--text-dark`, `--brand-primary`, `--brand-blue`, `--brand-teal` and `--brand-gold` were referenced without fallbacks in `style.css`, `error_400.html`, `admin_attendance_log.html` and `system_admin_network_activity.html`. An unresolvable `var()` with no fallback makes the declaration invalid at computed-value time, so the property was silently dropped and the element inherited — the `.bg-student` / `.text-student` / `.border-student` utilities applied no color at all, and the sysadmin network-activity stat numbers rendered as body text. This is the failure mode the token layer is supposed to make impossible, and nothing errored. All eight now resolve to real tokens, verified by a whole-tree sweep that reports zero unresolvable references.

- **`--warning-text` was a token that never existed (2026-09-11)** — `.bg-warning-subtle` read `var(--warning-text, #856404)`, so it had always silently rendered the fallback; the named token was defined nowhere in the repository. It now uses `--alert-warning-text`.

- **Teacher `--bs-secondary-rgb` disagreed with `--bs-secondary` (2026-09-11)** — The RGB triple still decomposed v1's `#d3af37` while the hex token had moved to `#D4A857`. Any `rgba(var(--bs-secondary-rgb), α)` therefore rendered a stale accent, without erroring, and only in translucent contexts. The student and sysadmin themes defined only `--bs-primary-rgb`, leaving the other five to fall through to Bootstrap's blue defaults. All three themes now carry the complete, parity-checked set.

- **`.btn-danger` ignored the role theme (2026-09-11)** — It was the one button rule still on hardcoded `#b3261e`/`#8f1f1f` while every sibling used tokens, so the destructive action was the single control that did not follow the theme. Now `var(--danger)`/`var(--danger-hover)`; verified 5.44:1 against white, unchanged from the value it replaced.

- **A gold stat number on the sysadmin network-activity page failed AA (2026-09-11)** — Sysadmin `--secondary-hover` is `#c09840`, 2.69:1 on white, which fails even the 3:1 large-text threshold — so the figure the page exists to display was the least legible thing on it. Text that must stay legible regardless of role is now pinned in role-agnostic tokens (`--accent-text-on-light` at 6.70:1, and the three `--alert-*-text` values), documented in `SPEC-DES-001` §VI.6. Per-role definitions were rejected deliberately: they reintroduce the same failure, silently, in whichever role picks a bad value. The adjacent `aria-hidden` icon keeps the theme token, as decoration carries no contrast obligation.

  Accessibility validation per `INV-ARC-020` §X: contrast recomputed from resolved values in-browser for all three role themes; `--accent-text-on-light` 6.70:1 and `--text-muted` 7.84:1 (improved from `#5f5f5c`'s 6.41:1) against `--surface`; teacher, student and sysadmin shells confirmed to resolve every token with no unresolved references and no console errors.

- **The student theme paired gold fills with a bronze-era text colour (2026-09-11)** — `--secondary` had converged on gold `#D4A857` in all three roles, but the student theme's companion tokens were never moved with it: `--secondary-text` was still `--text-inverse` and `--secondary-hover` was still the brown `#725132`, and the comment above them literally read "White text on brown". Every shared component that consumes the pair — `.bg-secondary`, `.btn-secondary`, and the badges built on them — therefore rendered white on gold at **2.20:1** in the student shell, and white on `#c09840` at **2.58:1** on hover.

  The teacher theme had the same defect on the hover leg and neither review caught it: `--secondary-text` `#171717` on `--secondary-hover` `#735816` is **2.75:1**. Rather than patch the one reported case, all three roles are now unified on the sysadmin triplet that was already correct — `#D4A857` / `#c09840` / `--neutral-900` — which is the only combination where both the rest and hover legs clear AA. `--secondary-hover` is consequently a *lighter* darkening than a brown would be; that is the constraint, not an oversight, and `SPEC-DES-001` §V.4 now states that the secondary triplet is role-agnostic and why: a role theme MUST NOT set a light `--secondary-text`, and MUST NOT darken `--secondary-hover` past the point where `--secondary-text` still clears AA on it.

  Measured after the change, in the student shell: `--secondary` + `--secondary-text` **8.14:1** (was 2.20), `--secondary-hover` + `--secondary-text` **6.66:1** (was 2.58).

- **Gold was used as body text on fourteen auth pages (2026-09-11)** — Forty `color: var(--secondary)` declarations across the sign-in, sign-up, credential and recovery templates set link, label, subtitle and helper text to the gold *fill*, which is **2.12:1** on `--surface` — below even the 3:1 large-text floor. `--accent-text-on-light` (`#735816`, 6.70:1) exists for exactly this and was simply not reached for. All forty now use it.

  Page-local `<style>` blocks are how the mistake kept recurring, so a floor was added to the shared shell rather than only to the templates named in review: `.auth-body a:not(.btn)` is specificity (0,3,1) and outranks a bare `a` selector, so a page that reaches for the accent again still renders the darkened variant. Buttons are excluded deliberately — `<a class="btn">` carries its own foreground against a filled background, and a bare `.auth-body a` would have turned white button labels dark gold. The `a:hover { opacity: 0.8 }` pattern is also gone: fading `#735816` lands at **4.21:1**, which fails AA on hover only, so hover is now signalled by `text-decoration: underline` at unchanged contrast.

  Accessibility validation per `INV-ARC-020` §X: a per-element audit walked every text-bearing node in `.auth-brand` and `.auth-body`, resolved each element's effective background by climbing ancestors past transparent fills, and applied the 3:1 or 4.5:1 threshold appropriate to its computed size and weight. **Zero failures** across `/student/login`, `/admin/login`, `/sysadmin/login`, `/student/claim-account`, `/admin/recover` and `/admin/signup`, covering all three role shells. `tests/test_accessibility.py` and `tests/test_axe_compliance.py` pass (21 tests).

- **The Turnstile widget was clipped out of the auth card on a phone (2026-09-11)** — Cloudflare's default "normal" widget is a fixed 300px wide and `.auth-container` sets `overflow: hidden`, so on a 320px viewport — where the card is 272px with 224px of content width — the bot check was cut off and the form could not be submitted at all. Widening the shell cannot fix it: reaching 300px of content at 320px leaves roughly 4px of gutter, and Cloudflare's `flexible` size still floors at 300px.

  The four pages that rendered `.cf-turnstile` by hand now call a single `macros/turnstile.html`, which asks for the documented 150px `compact` size below `26rem`. The size has to be set before `api.js` renders the widget; `api.js` is `async defer` in `<head>` and therefore runs after parsing, so the macro's inline script always wins that race. Centralizing it is the point — a fifth page cannot reintroduce the bug by copying a `<div>`, and `SPEC-DES-001` §IX now prohibits hand-written `.cf-turnstile` markup.

  Verified by measuring the shell rather than the widget, since no site key is configured in this environment: at a 320px viewport a 300px element overflows `.auth-container` and a 150px one does not, and the `26rem` threshold is correctly placed — at 417px the media query stops matching and content width is 321px, so the normal widget fits unscaled.

- **The selected page in the docs sidebar was the hardest one to read (2026-09-11)** — `.docs-sidebar .nav-link.active` put gold on the `--primary` fill at `0.9rem`, which is normal text and needs 4.5:1; it measured **4.34:1** in the teacher theme and **3.75:1** in the student theme. Gold is legitimate against a dark fill only where the large-text threshold applies. The label is now `--text-inverse`; the fill already carries the selected state, so nothing is lost. The two remaining gold-on-`--primary` uses in `getting_started_widget.html` were checked and kept: one is an `aria-hidden` icon and the other is bold `h5`, both large text clearing 3:1.

- **Bootstrap hex literals and inline styles survived in the auth templates (2026-09-11)** — The passphrase strength meter in `student_pin_setup.html` carried five hardcoded Bootstrap palette hexes in JavaScript, so the one piece of feedback that tells a student whether their credential is safe was the one piece that ignored the theme. It now uses `--danger` / `--warning` / `--success`; the five zxcvbn steps are still distinguishable because the *bar's width* carries the gradation and the fill only has to say bad / borderline / good. The sign-in wait modal's inline spinner and tip dimensions became `.auth-loading-spinner` / `.auth-loading-tip` on the shared scale, and a stray inline icon `font-size` in `system_admin_login.html` became `.icon-lg`.

- **Submitting the default rebalance activation mode raised instead of scheduling anything (2026-09-11)** — The deferred mode is the form's default and the route's own default, and it could not be submitted. Two independent defects sat on that one path. `apply_economy_rebalance` passed its `FeatureSettings` row into the `class_id` parameter of `queue_scheduled_policy_transitions`, which binds that value into `PolicyVersion.class_id` and `PolicyTransition.class_id`; and the row was fetched with `create=True`, so for any class without one the getter flushed a new `FeatureSettings` outside a FEAT context and `enforce_feat_context_on_flush` raised `FEATContextError` before the first defect could even be reached. Both are gone: the lookup is deleted rather than repaired, because `_apply_rebalance_plan` only ever read `class_id` off the row, and `selected_scope['class_id']` — already authority-checked — carries it. Pinning the rendered radio could not see any of this, so `tests/test_economic_engine_rebalance_activation.py` now drives the POST and asserts a `PolicyTransition` exists; verified failing against both defects.

- **The student dashboard printed a savings balance its own interest projection contradicted (2026-09-11)** — The card shows the *available* balance (posted + pending) while `SPEC-ECON-001` §9.2 accrues on the *posted* balance alone, so between a deposit and the next settlement sweep the page carried a $50.00 card directly above a projection computed on $0.00, with nothing reconciling them. The dashboard now discloses the gap in the same words the transfer page already uses, on the same condition, and states that interest is earned on the posted figure only. Covered by `tests/test_dashboard_savings_projection_disclosure.py`, which also asserts the disclosure disappears once settlement makes the two agree.

- **The delete-gate countdown announced itself as incomplete after completing (2026-09-11)** — `updateCountdownUi` tracked `aria-valuenow` against the bar's width while counting, then set the width to 100% at completion without updating the value, leaving the progressbar announcing its last intermediate percentage. A screen-reader user was told the countdown was still running while the confirmation controls were live (WCAG 2.1.1).

- **Rent was judged against a band boundary the teacher could not type (2026-09-11)** — `check_rent_balance` derived the recommended band as an unrounded product of CWI and the policy ratio, then compared the saved rent against it. At CWI 337.50 the minimum is `$880.5133928571428`, so a rent of `$880.51` was reported as *below* a minimum that prints as the same number — and the next amount the teacher could actually enter, `$880.52`, was the only satisfying one. The Economic Engine showed "Pricing slightly off" for an amount it had itself recommended.

  Money in this application is cents-denominated, so a boundary expressed in fractional cents is not a band the teacher can land on. Both comparison sites now quantize the band to cents before comparing: `check_rent_balance`, and `validate_rent_value`, which had the same shape in a worse form — it compared raw ratios while quoting a 2-dp band back in its own warning text, so the message named a boundary identical to the value it was rejecting. Verified against the reported class: the same `$880.51` now reports `info | Rent is balanced at $880.51/month (2.61x CWI) | min 880.51 max 1100.64`. `tests/test_pricing_recommendation_single_source.py::test_recommended_minimum_is_accepted_as_balanced` covers every policy mode × billing frequency × CWI combination (105 passed) and was proven load-bearing — restoring either old comparison fails 66 of them.

- **One policy ratio produced several different prices (2026-09-11)** — Fixing the band boundary above exposed that the boundary was not the only disagreement: the same policy ratio reached the teacher through four separate derivations, and they did not agree.

  `AVERAGE_WEEKS_PER_MONTH` had three spellings — `economy_policy.AVERAGE_WEEKS_PER_MONTH`, a re-stated literal in `FREQUENCY_WEEK_MULTIPLIERS["monthly"]`, and a float `365.25 / 12 / 7` on `EconomyBalanceChecker` — so a monthly band depended on which module computed it. The Economic Engine card built its monthly band by scaling an already-rounded weekly band, while the rent page scaled the raw ratio and rounded once, which is a different number. `check_rent_balance` converted the teacher's rent to a monthly equivalent before judging it, adding a rounding step the page never took, so a value copied off the page could come back flagged. `validate_fine_value` read the default-mode constants regardless of the class's policy mode, so a tight or comfortable class was shown its own band and judged against another's. And store tiers were matched on the raw ratio while the tiers were printed as cents, so a price typed from a tier's own printed maximum fell a fraction of a cent outside it and was rejected as unaffordable.

  `economy_policy` now owns the month length as a single `Decimal` that the multiplier table and the checker class both reference by identity, plus `weeks_per_period`, `frequency_label` and `scale_band` — the last of which rounds exactly once, because rounding then scaling is not scaling then rounding. `EconomyBalanceChecker` derives every recommendation through `rent_band` / `fine_band` / `store_tier_bands`, the warning and the settings page call the same one, and the warning judges rent in the cadence the teacher set it in rather than in a monthly equivalent nobody sees. `tests/test_pricing_recommendation_single_source.py` covers 3 policy modes × 7 billing frequencies × 5 CWI values (274 passed); before the refactor 136 of them failed.

  The rebalance preview was a fourth derivation, and the one with teeth: `_build_rebalance_preview` scaled `rent_weekly.recommended` — already rounded to cents — into the teacher's cadence through `convert_weekly_amount_to_frequency`, so the amount the "Apply" button would *write* could differ by a cent from the amount the rent page recommends. It now reads `rent_band(...)['recommended']`. Its private `_format_frequency_label` (a third spelling of the cadence wording, which rendered `$880.51 / monthly`) is replaced by `frequency_label`. `convert_weekly_amount_to_frequency` had no remaining caller and is deleted rather than left available, because scaling an already-rounded figure is the defect itself. `test_rebalance_preview_proposes_the_band_the_page_recommends` covers the same 105 combinations and was verified load-bearing: restoring the old scaling fails 45 of them.

  The client was the fifth derivation and the one a teacher actually looked at. `displayCWIInfo` in `static/js/economy-balance.js` took the weekly band out of the analyze payload and multiplied it — `× 2` for biweekly, `÷ 7` for daily, `× value` for custom — so at CWI 999.99 the screen read `$1199.98` where the server's biweekly band is `$1199.99`, and it recovered the "% of CWI" line by dividing the rounded dollar band back out because the ratio was not in the payload. The drift was known: `templates/admin_rent_settings.html` set `suppressRecommendationsEcho: true` with a comment naming "the rounding-drift between the two calculation paths", which hid the contradiction instead of resolving it. The page now asks `/admin/api/economy/validate/rent` for the band and its cadence wording — both from `rent_band` and `frequency_label` — and the policy ratios are exposed on the analyze payload so the percentage is read rather than reverse-engineered. `INV-ARC-022` requires the value shown and the value judged to come from one source; there are no longer two.

- **The student dashboard advertised 4.5% interest the class would never pay (2026-09-11)** — The savings card computed its forecast as `savings_balance * 0.045 / 12` inline. Every part of that was wrong independently: a hardcoded APY inside a projection, which `SPEC-ECON-001` §10 forbids because a projection must use execution's rules; a rate no class configures, where §11 forbids a hidden default APY, so the honest forecast for a default classroom is nothing at all; the *available* balance rather than the posted one accrual actually uses (§9.2); and bespoke arithmetic that ignored the class's calculation type, compounding and payout cadence while labelling the result "/month" regardless of the configured cadence. The transfer page had already been repaired for the same sections and the dashboard was left behind.

  The forecast now comes from `savings_interest_for_payout_period` over `get_posted_balance` with the class's resolved policy, captioned with the real payout cadence, and the whole block is suppressed when no rate is configured rather than inventing one. A dead `apply_savings_interest` wrapper carrying its own `annual_rate=Decimal('0.045')` default is deleted along with its re-export. `tests/test_dashboard_savings_forecast.py` asserts both halves — that an unconfigured class is shown no projection line, and that the rendered figure is the engine's.

- **The rebalance form named a cadence unrelated to when the change lands (2026-09-11)** — Rent is assessed per bill cycle, and a deferred rebalance is scheduled by `_get_rent_effective_at` from the *rent* timeline: the cycle after the upcoming bill. The route's own default is `REBALANCE_ACTIVATION_NEXT_RENEWAL` and its flash already said "the renewal after the upcoming bill". The form nonetheless offered "Next Payroll Run (Recommended)" and submitted `next_payroll` — naming payroll, which has nothing to do with rent assessment. The label now reads "Next Cycle (Recommended)" and submits `next_renewal`, and a `form-text` states the rule the teacher needs: rent takes effect on the cycle after the upcoming bill, store prices change immediately because they are not cycle-based.

  Because that moved submissions out of the one mode the `effective_at is None` fallback in `activate_due_rebalances` covered, the fallback now covers every non-immediate mode — otherwise a deferred transition whose effective date cannot be computed has no later moment to wait for and sits pending forever.

- **The immediate-change warning was shown beside a choice the teacher had not made (2026-09-11)** — The warning that applying a rebalance mid-cycle re-prices rent part-way through it, and its "I understand" confirmation checkbox, rendered unconditionally next to a radio group whose default is deferred. A warning attached to nothing is read as noise, which is how a real one stops being read. Both now live in a `hidden` panel that appears only when "Apply Immediately" is selected, and the confirmation is cleared when the panel hides so a teacher cannot leave a stale acknowledgement behind by changing their mind. `tests/test_economic_engine_rebalance_activation.py` pins the rendered default and the hidden panel; both were confirmed failing against the old markup.

- **An attendance tap raised before it could record anything (2026-09-11)** — `handle_tap` carried `@requires_feat_context("FEAT-PROD-001")` and called `record_attendance_session`, which carries the same decorator. Exactly one FEAT executes per invocation (`INV-ARC-000` §VIII.2), so the inner envelope raised `FEATContextError`. This is the same defect class already fixed in `enforce_daily_limits_job`; the route-level decorator is removed, since the route's own work — PIN verification and resolution — is reads only.

- **"Time Today" was wrong until the first poll (2026-09-11)** — `attendance.js` bootstraps the UI from a server-rendered state blob and then refreshes it every ten seconds. The refresh path already read `duration_today` via `pickTimeToday`, but the bootstrap read `initialState.duration` directly, so a student opening the page saw session duration under a "Time Today" heading for up to ten seconds before it silently corrected itself. Both paths now go through the same accessor.

- **The admin store page raised `NameError` on recent purchases (2026-09-11)** — `derive_display_status` was referenced in `app/routes/admin.py` but never imported. It had also been defined privately in `app/routes/api.py`, so the function a route blueprint needed lived inside another route blueprint; it and its three lineage-query helpers now live in `app/services/entitlement_read_service.py`, where a read-model derivation belongs, and both blueprints import it from there.

- **No ledger effect ever posted, because nothing in the running application settled the ledger (2026-09-11)** — `create_pending_transaction` is the sole ledger write boundary and it creates every effect `PENDING`. `settle_balances` is the only thing that admits an effect to posted history: it assigns `posting_sequence` and advances `LedgerBalanceSnapshot`. Between those two facts sat the defect — no route, service, FEAT or scheduled job ever called settlement. A sweep function existed (`settle_pending_transaction_contexts`, with its own per-context `FEAT-LED-003` boundary and failure isolation), and a CLI wrapper existed (`scripts/settle_pending_transactions.py`), and the sweep's own docstring anticipated "the standalone scheduled sweep". Nothing scheduled it.

  So in production no transaction ever reached `POSTED`, not one `LedgerBalanceSnapshot` row was ever created, and every posted-balance read answered zero permanently. Savings interest accrues on posted balances only (`SPEC-ECON-001` §9.2), so no student could earn interest — the accrual base was structurally pinned at zero. The visible symptom was a student's savings card reading $50.00 directly above a projection captioned "based on current posted balance of $0.00", flat across twelve months. Both reads were individually correct; the ledger beneath them had never settled.

  `ledger_settlement` is now a registered hourly job. Because `savings_interest_payout` is also hourly and two interval jobs have no ordering guarantee between them — and because an unsettled base makes interest *underpay silently* rather than fail — `run_savings_interest_job` discharges the dependency itself by settling first, rather than trusting registration order.

  The reason the suite never caught it is worth recording: `tests/helpers/ledger.py` deliberately exposes `settle_ledger_balances`, so every balance test supplied the trigger production was missing. Every such test was green *because the test settled on its own behalf*. And nothing in `tests/` referenced `init_scheduled_tasks`, `add_job` or `get_jobs` at all, so job registration was wholly unguarded — which is how a correct function nobody scheduled reached a deployment candidate. `init_scheduled_tasks` is skipped under `TESTING`, so registration could not be asserted without starting a real `BackgroundScheduler` in pytest. The ten inline `scheduler.add_job` calls are therefore replaced by a declared `SCHEDULED_JOB_SPECS` tuple that `init_scheduled_tasks` iterates, making membership, trigger and ordering assertable without a scheduler. New `tests/test_ledger_settlement_job.py` (8 passed) drives the job rather than the helper, and its three registration and ordering guards were verified load-bearing by removing the spec and the ordering call, which fails them.

- **The savings page printed two different balances and reconciled neither (2026-09-11)** — The savings card reads posted + pending; the projection caption and the interest forecast read posted alone. Both are correct — `SPEC-ECON-001` §9.2 makes accrual posted-only and §10 requires the projection to use the same rule as execution — but the page quoted both numbers with nothing connecting them. With settlement now running the gap is a short window rather than permanent, and it is still real: between a deposit and the next sweep a student sees a card reading $50.00 above a caption stating their posted balance is $0.00.

  The caption now names the card's figure and says why it differs, and only when it actually differs. Pinned by `tests/test_transfer_savings_projection_route.py::test_unsettled_savings_are_disclosed_rather_than_left_contradicting`, which asserts both directions — the disclosure appears while the deposit is pending and is gone once settlement makes the two figures agree — so the condition is proven to track state rather than being permanently on.

- **Eleven student pages had a help panel that said nothing (2026-09-10)** — The teacher shell got this treatment first; the student shell was left with the contract and none of the content. Eleven of the twelve templates extending `layout_student.html` overrode no `contextual_help_body` and opened to the placeholder sentence.

  The student case was the worse of the two. `layout_admin.html` renders its "Need Help?" trigger only when a doc path resolves, so a teacher page with nothing to say showed no button at all. `layout_student.html` renders the trigger unconditionally and forces a fallback `help_doc_path`, so every one of those pages offered help and then answered with a placeholder — spending a student's trust on a dead end, for the audience least able to route around it.

  Panels are now written for rent, finances, payroll, store, insurance marketplace, policy detail, claim filing, issue reporting, help & support, adding a class, and teacher-recovery verification. The recovery panel is deliberately blunt about social engineering: a student who is asked out of band to confirm a "teacher" recovery is being attacked, and the panel says to stop and tell an adult. `tests/test_docs_help_links.py::test_every_student_page_explains_itself` now fails if a student template is added without one.

  The rent page was found late, and by fixing the guard rather than by reading the templates. `_templates_extending` matched only `extends "layout_student.html"` — the double-quoted form — while `student_rent.html` uses single quotes, which Jinja accepts identically. So the one student page the guard could not see was the one page still shipping the placeholder, and the guard reported the portal clean. It now matches both quote styles, which is a reminder that a green guard whose selector is narrower than its subject is indistinguishable from a satisfied one.

  The claim-filing panel was also split by claim type. It described date and hours fields and a filing deadline as though they were universal; `student_file_claim.html` renders those fields only for productivity policies, and the deadline exists only when the policy declares `claim_window_days`. A transaction claim now gets guidance about choosing the transaction it attaches to, and the deadline topic renders only for a policy that has one — help must describe the form on the screen, not a sibling form.

- **A posted ledger row could be walked backwards through its lifecycle (2026-09-10)** — `_guard_ledger_immutability` enforces `INV-LED-002` over the monetary and identity facts, and deliberately left `status` alone because settlement genuinely rewrites it. But "settlement writes it" is not "anything may write it": `ledger_settlement_service` is the only post-insert writer and only ever moves `PENDING` to `POSTED`, and `INV-LED-003` requires a void or reversal to arrive as a **new linked row** rather than as an edit to the original. `POSTED` → `PENDING`, anything → `VOID`, and a revived `VOID` were all accepted — each one a rewrite of a settled fact wearing a lifecycle costume, and the last two would have changed a balance without leaving a correction behind.

  The guard now rejects every status write except the single lawful forward transition. This was found through its own test: `test_INV_LED_002__status_still_advances_through_settlement` seeded a **posted** row, set it back to `PENDING`, and asserted the reversal succeeded — so the one test named for the lawful transition was passing *because* the unlawful one was permitted, and would have kept passing no matter how the guard drifted. It now seeds `PENDING` and asserts `POSTED`, with a parametrized sibling pinning all four rejected directions.

- **Ledger immutability was enforced only as deep as the ORM (2026-09-10)** — `_guard_ledger_immutability` is a SQLAlchemy `before_update` listener, and a mapper-level listener fires only for ORM unit-of-work flushes. A Core `update()`, a bulk `Query.update()`, `db.session.execute(text(...))`, a migration, a maintenance script, or a `psql` session all bypassed it completely. This was not hypothetical: this branch shipped a fixture in `tests/test_insurance_transaction_economics.py` that backdated a posted row by going around the mapper *on purpose*, with a comment saying so. A guard whose own test suite documents the way around it is a convention, not an invariant.

  `DOM-LED-001 §VII` states the protected fields are immutable "once inserted", without qualifying by access path, so migration `f1a9c3e60b72` moves the enforcement to where the write lands. `ledger_transaction_no_rewrite` mirrors the ORM guard in plpgsql: the monetary and identity facts may not change at all; the write-once columns may go `NULL` → value exactly once; `status` may only advance `PENDING` → `POSTED`. `feat_code` stays free, because `_enforce_transaction_integrity` restamps it with the FEAT performing the lawful update.

  The insurance fixture is fixed rather than exempted. It needed a loss dated eight days ago, and the Ledger posting boundary accepts no caller-supplied time on purpose — when a monetary effect happened is a fact the Ledger stamps. So the fixture now moves the clock the insert reads instead of rewriting the row afterwards, which keeps the fiction in the test's notion of "now" and leaves the row immutable from the moment it exists.

  `INV-ARC-017` notes, both scope limits recorded rather than glossed:

  - This covers `UPDATE` only, and that is the correct and complete scope, now written down as `DOM-LED-001 §VII.2`. The question a `DELETE` trigger raised — whether removing a seat may erase that seat's ledger history — was initially surfaced as an open finding, then resolved at the domain level rather than in a migration. Immutability governs *mutation of a surviving class universe*; cascade deletion arising from lawful seat or class destruction is lifecycle destruction, not an edit to surviving financial history. There is never a lawful state in which the seat is gone but the seat's money remains, so a general `DELETE` prohibition would assert the inverse rule — that ledger history must outlive the entity whose existence gives it meaning — and would abort lawful actor removal.
  - A related concern was raised and then disproved by reachability rather than by assertion: `actor_seat_id` cascades, and a teacher seat can be the actor on rows owned by student seats, which looked like a partial-destruction hazard. It is not reachable. `classes.teacher_user_id → users ON DELETE CASCADE`, so a teacher principal cannot outlive its classes; both live call sites of `delete_seat_with_profile` reject a non-student seat (`remove_pending_student_seat`, FEAT-CLASS-002); and the third reference, `app/routes/admin.py:141`, is a dead import. Teacher-attributed rows are reachable by cascade only when the whole class universe is already being destroyed. `actor_seat_id` / `target_seat_id` are provenance and participation references; `seat_id` carries economic ownership.
  - The trigger immediately caught a real latent defect in the deletion path, which is the first evidence that it enforces something the ORM guard did not. `app/utils/student_deletion.py` swept `ledger_transaction.original_transaction_id` and `reversal_transaction_id` to `NULL` on any surviving row referencing a transaction about to be deleted, through a bulk `Query.update()` — a Core UPDATE the mapper listener never saw. Under the trigger that raises, so **any student who had ever been refunded could not be deleted at all**. The sweep is removed rather than exempted, because the state it defended against is unreachable: neither column is FK-enforced, and a reversal pair can never span economic owners. `ledger_correction_service.reverse_transaction` builds the reversal by copying `seat_id`/`class_id`/`target_seat_id` from the original (only `actor_seat_id`, provenance, may differ), and the insurance-reimbursement path writes `seat_id=student_seat.id` behind `insurance_eligibility_contract`'s `transaction.seat_id != covered_seat_id` rejection; every other writer merely forwards a caller-supplied value. So lawful seat deletion always takes both ends of the pair in one cascade, and clearing the pointers would have been a rewrite of surviving financial history — the exact mutation §VII.2 forbids — performed in the name of referential tidiness on columns no constraint checks. What the function does still do is load-bearing and unchanged: `issues.related_transaction_id` and `issue_resolution_actions.related_transaction_id` *are* real foreign keys with `ON DELETE NO ACTION`, and `issues` carries no class or seat scope of its own, so those rows do not cascade away with the seat and the delete aborts without the sweep. It is renamed `_clear_support_transaction_refs` to say which of the two it is. New `tests/dom/ledger/test_reversal_ownership_and_deletion.py` (2 passed) pins both halves — that a reversal inherits its original's owner, and that deleting a refunded student removes both rows and raises nothing — and the second was verified load-bearing by restoring the sweep, which fails it with `RaiseException: ledger_transaction.original_transaction_id is immutable (INV-LED-002)` from `prevent_ledger_transaction_rewrite`. No existing test covered a student with a reversed transaction, which is why 33 deletion tests passed over the defect.
  - The database-level tests assert through raw SQL deliberately. An ORM-driven assertion would pass against an ORM-only guard and prove nothing about the property being claimed. A drift test also pins the trigger's column lists against `_LEDGER_IMMUTABLE_FIELDS` / `_LEDGER_WRITE_ONCE_FIELDS`, so opening a column at one layer and not the other fails a test instead of producing a write one layer permits and the other rejects.

- **A migration downgrade restored a different column than it removed (2026-09-10)** — `a4f2b7c91d38` drops `payroll_settings.bonus_rate`, but its `downgrade()` re-added the column with `server_default='0.0'`. The original column (`i6j7k8l9m0n1`) declared no server default. A rollback that leaves the schema in a state the forward history never produced is not a rollback, and on an append-only versioned policy table it would have silently started stamping a default onto rows that previously took `NULL`. The default is removed.

- **The teacher insurance claim list was labelled "Pending Claims" while showing every claim (2026-09-10)** — `list_insurance_claims` applies no status filter, so the list includes approved and denied claims. The heading is restored to "Filed claims". Filtering the query instead would have been a behavior change smuggled in under a copy fix, and the all-status view is the one the page has always provided.

- **`/health/status` documented probes it never ran (2026-09-10)** — The docstring claimed the endpoint checked the "Seat table", the "Administrator table" and the "Hall passes table". It checks none of them, and `administrators` has not existed since the v2 identity migration. The endpoint executes exactly one probe, `SELECT 1`, and reports every other signal as `UNKNOWN` / `CHECK_NOT_REGISTERED`. The docstring now says that, and names it as the contract rather than a gap: a capability stays UNKNOWN until a lawful read-only probe is registered, so the endpoint cannot imply health it has not observed (`INV-ARC-017`).

- **Nineteen teacher pages had a help panel that said nothing (2026-09-10)** — `layout_admin.html` renders a shared contextual-help offcanvas, but only the ten pages that declared an offcanvas of their own carried real guidance. Every other teacher page opened to a single generic sentence and two resource links: a panel that answered no question the teacher could have had while looking at it.

  The shared offcanvas body is now an overridable `contextual_help_body` block, so a page contributes prose without duplicating the offcanvas scaffold. Nineteen pages were written against what the page actually does — insurance authoring and claims, payroll history, store item editing, analytics, customizations, feature settings, the two deletion surfaces, passkeys, recovery, support tickets, issue review, student detail, announcements, and the dashboard. The generic sentence survives only as the fallback for a page that has not been written yet.

  The analytics panel is deliberately framed as prompts rather than findings, per `DOM-ITR`: the page observes and does not prescribe, and its help must not turn an observation into a verdict. `tests/test_docs_help_links.py::test_every_admin_page_explains_itself` now fails if any teacher template overrides neither `contextual_help_body` nor `contextual_help_trigger`, so a page added later cannot quietly reintroduce the blank panel.

- **Class deletion moved out of the global account surface (2026-09-10)** — The Account Deletion page under Settings offered two destruction targets: the teacher's account *and* the active class. Settings is the global authenticated-principal surface; a class is one tenant. Mixing them put a class-scoped irreversible action on a page whose whole subject is the user principal, where a teacher reaches it without ever having chosen which class they are standing in.

  Class deletion now lives at `/admin/class-delete` under Class Tools, alongside the other active-class surfaces, and redirects to the dashboard when no class is active. Account deletion keeps its own page and its own gate. The destruction authority itself did not change: `admin.delete_join_code` already resolved its target from the canonical context alone and explicitly ignored any client-supplied `join_code` — this was a placement defect, not an authority one.

  The timed gate (30s countdown, typed phrase, 10s continuous hold) was extracted into `templates/components/timed_delete_gate.html` and `static/js/timed-delete-gate.js` so both surfaces mount one implementation rather than a copy. The gate payload carries proof of intent only and no deletion target, preserving `INV-CORE-000` §III.1/§III.4. `tests/dom/identity/test_destruction_authority_boundary.py` now pins the split: no class target on the account page, no account target on the class page, a redirect without active class context, and each link confined to its own nav group.

- **Decorative icons no longer read their glyph names aloud (2026-09-09)** — 819 `material-symbols-outlined` spans across 66 templates carried no `aria-hidden`. Material Symbols is a ligature font, so a screen reader announced the literal ligature — "quick_reference_all", "arrow_back" — as page content interleaved with the real text (WCAG 4.1.2, `INV-ARC-020`). The three authenticated layouts already swept the DOM for this at runtime, which hid the defect on inherited pages but did nothing for standalone documents, for the window before scripts run, or for the 15 icons that live inside `innerHTML` strings.

  Two icons were the *sole* content of a control, where hiding alone would have left the control unnamed: the greeting link on the admin dashboard and the docs search submit button. Both relied on a Bootstrap `data-bs-title` tooltip, which is not an accessible name. They were given `aria-label` first, before any icon was hidden.

  `tests/test_layout_accessibility_contract.py` now walks every template with a Jinja-aware tag scanner — a `<span[^>]*>` regex mis-splits `class="{{ 'a' if x > y else 'b' }}"`, and BeautifulSoup cannot see inside `<script>` — and fails on any undecorated icon, plus a second test pinning the two accessible names. Both were confirmed to go red with the fix removed.

- **The "Need Help?" button now reports whether its panel is open (2026-09-10)** — The contextual-help trigger in both the admin and student layouts declared `aria-controls` for a real offcanvas panel but carried no `aria-expanded`, so a screen-reader user was never told the panel had opened (WCAG 4.1.2, `INV-ARC-020`). This was the single failing test in the full 2062-test suite; because `constitutional-ci.yml` is diff-scoped and `schema-gate.yml` runs only `-m "critical or regression"`, nothing ran it unless those templates changed, and `main` sat red while CI reported green. `layout_admin.html` is extended by 28 templates and `layout_student.html` by the student portal, so the defect reached nearly every authenticated page.

  Both triggers now ship `aria-expanded="false"`, and the new `static/js/offcanvas-aria.js` keeps the attribute in step with the panel — Bootstrap manages the panel but does not touch the trigger, so a static attribute alone would have been a frozen value rather than a fix. The module binds every `[data-bs-toggle="offcanvas"][aria-controls]` trigger, so future offcanvas surfaces are covered without further edits. Verified in a real browser against Bootstrap 5.3.3: the state tracks open, close-button, and Escape.

  `tests/test_layout_accessibility_contract.py` now runs against both layouts instead of the admin one alone, matches the expanded-state value by shape rather than an enumerated list of Jinja expressions, and asserts that any layout carrying offcanvas triggers also loads the sync module.


- **Rollback instructions name an explicit revision (2026-09-09)** — The documented rollback and downgrade-test procedures called a bare `flask db downgrade`. The current migration head is a merge point with two parents, so that form aborts with `Ambiguous walk` and rolls nothing back. `.claude/rules/database-migrations.md` and the `CLAUDE.md` command reference now use `flask db downgrade <revision>` and say why. The CI schema gate already passed an explicit revision and was unaffected.

- **Archived documents no longer claim authority (2026-09-09)** — 46 documents under `docs/archive/` declared `Authority Level: Normative` (44) or `Foundational` (2), contradicting `.claude/rules/documentation.md`, which states that archived material is "history only, never authority". Their `Authority Level` now reads `Archived - non-normative`, and each carries a one-line notice pointing at the current `INV` / `DOM` / `SOP` trees. Live documents were not touched, nor were body-table rows that cite a still-current document's authority.

- **Archived v1 attack-surface audit is marked as history (2026-09-09)** — `SEC-AUD-012` carried remediation shell that writes secrets into a `.env` file for a `deploy.yml` workflow that no longer exists, presented as normative guidance. It now opens with an archive banner stating it is v1 history, that its shell must not be run, and where the current secret-provisioning and security contracts live.

- **Hall Pass setup has one UI surface (2026-09-08)** — Removed the obsolete standalone setup template, its admin route, and its endpoint mappings. Destinations are configured in Hall Pass Management's Configure tab; its setup API remains available.

- **Hall Pass Out Limit saves and reloads the selected value (2026-09-08)** — The management page now uses `max_queue_limit` consistently with the settings API. Removed the unsupported granting switch, rejected missing or invalid save values instead of silently saving 10, and updated the teacher guide.

- **Hall Pass Configure component cleanup (2026-09-08)** — Removed conditional page shells and duplicate dependencies from the configuration component, restored CTH section headers in the tab, and aligned accessible limit names and teacher guidance with approval checks. Payroll troubleshooting now directs teachers to Support for incorrect posted payments instead of editing immutable attendance history. Focused rendering checks are recorded separately from browser accessibility certification.

- **Reversals now persist the canonical ledger type and refuse a second application (2026-09-08)** — A compensating transaction records `REVERSAL` in `type` per `FEAT-LED-002` §III.2.1, with the business reason moved to the new `ledger_transaction.compensation_subtype` column. `reverse_transaction` also runs the reversal-authorization guard before any ledger mutation and rejects reversing a transaction that already carries one, so `INV-LED-013` / `INV-OPS-005` hold for every caller rather than per-caller.

- **Voiding a purchase resolves its grants by provenance, not by parsing the description (2026-09-08)** — `_void_purchase` now finds the granted units through the transaction's `correlation_id`. Product names carry no uniqueness constraint, so the old name lookup could revoke a student's units of the wrong lineage; and the `(xN)` in the description counted purchases rather than granted units, leaving bundle purchases partially reversible.

- **Insurance coverage is resolved by policy, so held coverage is visible again (2026-09-08)** — The policy page looked up entitlements with the policy uuid as `EntitlementEvent.product_id`, which holds a store product lineage. It matched no rows, so every holder of valid coverage was reported as uncovered and the claims table widened to every policy the seat held.

- **Non-consuming hall-pass logs no longer claim an unconsumed grant (2026-09-08)** — `hall_pass_id` stays NULL where there is no entitlement lifecycle, instead of naming a grant that remains available for a later consuming approval and copying that grant's correlation onto the approval.

- **A lapsed collective goal reaches its terminal state even with no open buy-ins (2026-09-08)** — Such a product stayed `IN_USE` and was re-evaluated on every hourly expiry run. Redemptions also no longer decrement goal progress: `CONSUMED` records that a met goal was honoured, not that a buy-in was withdrawn.

- **Store inventory no longer over-sells after a revoked grant or perk (2026-09-08)** — The `REVOKED` leg of `units_sold` is now restricted to `PURCHASE` acquisitions, matching the `GRANTED` leg it subtracts from.

- **A whole-class collective goal can be published (2026-09-08)** — Publication required a `collective_goal_target` for every collective item, while a whole-class goal derives its target from class size and leaves the field blank.

- **Conditional store-item validators now run (2026-09-08)** — Rent-linked, bundle, and bulk-discount quantity checks moved out of per-field hooks, which `Optional()` short-circuited for exactly the blank values they existed to catch.

- **Redemption requests can be re-submitted after a rejection (2026-09-08)** — The request correlation is derived from the attempt, so it no longer collides with the retained resolved row on `PendingAction.correlation_id`.

- **Fixed a `NameError` in the redemption display-status API (2026-09-08)** — `PendingAction` was referenced but never imported in `app/routes/api.py`.

- **Repaired the Redemption Audit query (2026-09-08)** — `Seat` was selected without a join, producing a cross join that duplicated every row and attached an arbitrary seat's identity to it. The teacher dashboard's pending-redemption list also now carries the class and `GRANTED` predicates its own count already used, so the two agree.

- **The economy rebalance job can run (2026-09-08)** — `FEAT-CLASS-005` is HIGH blast radius and its decorator-owned envelope refused before the body ran; each teacher now gets its own context, keyed and error-bounded, so one failure no longer rolls back the activations before it.

- **Sysadmin report updates commit (2026-09-08)** — A direct `db.session.commit()` inside the `FEAT-OPS-001` envelope tripped the atomicity guard and rolled the update back, and authorization now runs outside the FEAT context. The status selector also preserves workflow states it does not own instead of silently resetting them to Open.

- **Hall-pass setup saves what the teacher typed (2026-09-08)** — Edits to the per-destination limit were discarded on save, the student destination modal read a payload key the API never sent, and a second limit field was offered that the runtime has no field for.

- **A principal ledger debit requires a caller-supplied idempotency key (2026-09-08)** — The seat+class fallback repeated across debits, so the reservation boundary replayed or refused the second one.

- **Support tickets are no longer duplicated by a retry (2026-09-08)** — The submission key is derived from the payload, as every other mutation in the module does, rather than from a fresh UUID.

- **Students without a PIN get a usable message on transfer (2026-09-08)** — Recovery clears `pin_hash`, after which every PIN was reported simply as incorrect.

- **The insurance marketplace renders (2026-09-08)** — The policy card reads `waiting_period_days`, which the view builder did not set, raising `UndefinedError` for the whole page.

### Changed

- **Cancelling insurance verifies the passphrase (2026-09-08)** — `FEAT-IDEN-002` §Credential boundary lists cancellation alongside purchase; the route previously took a bare confirmation.

- **`store_products.item_type` is a closed vocabulary (2026-09-08)** — Enforced at the publication seam and by a new check constraint. An unmapped value published cleanly and then failed every policy read for the whole class.

- **Repaired the Alembic revision graph (2026-09-08)** — Assigned the hall-pass migration a unique revision ID, restored a single migration head, and updated the identity redirect test to use the canonical passphrase login contract.

- **Formalized the student credential boundary (2026-09-08)** — Passphrases now authenticate login and irreversible expense actions; PINs protect non-monetary or reversible actions, including transfers between checking and savings. Runtime prompts and student guides were aligned with the `FEAT-IDEN-002` matrix, including the insurance guide, which still described buying as a single unauthenticated click.

- **Completed Redemption Audit outcome filtering (2026-09-08)** — The store audit now derives its visible action from the retained request outcome and correctly filters `REQUEST`, `APPROVED`, and `REJECTED` history.

- **Added the self-hosting documentation destination (2026-09-08)** — `docs/self-hosting/README.md` now gives operators a safe orientation to the canonical deployment, security, runtime-boundary, evidence, and recovery SOPs.

- **Made the economic rebalance scope explicit (2026-09-08)** — The preview is now labeled **Rent Rebalance Preview**, the guide describes the working scheduled-activation path, and insurance, fees, and store pricing are explicitly directed to their own settings surfaces in accordance with `SPEC-ECON-003`.

- **Moved sysadmin issue updates behind the Operations FEAT boundary (2026-09-08)** — Report status changes now use canonical issue history, persist Operations review notes, and commit atomically under `FEAT-OPS-001`.

- **Retired hollow standalone sysadmin log pages (2026-09-08)** — `/sysadmin/error-logs`, `/sysadmin/network-activity`, and `/sysadmin/logs-testing` now redirect to the canonical combined-logs surface instead of rendering empty result sets.

- **Aligned student PIN surfaces and removed dead admin navigation (2026-09-08)** — Credential setup now enforces and displays the canonical 4–6 digit `FEAT-IDEN-002` range, and the unreferenced v1 `admin_nav.html` was removed.

- **Reorganized the user-guide coverage tracker’s open work (2026-09-08)** — Added a status table separating runtime defects, documentation/wiring work, tracker reconciliation, and required evidence so unresolved work is visible at a glance.

- **Added regression coverage for store and rent-link invariants (2026-09-08)** — Tests now pin single-unit checkout rejection, verify that editing a versioned product preserves derived stock and collective-goal progress through the stable product lineage, and ensure adding a rent-linked benefit does not rewrite an open bill cycle.

- **Removed stale guide claims from fixed surfaces (2026-09-08)** — The student dashboard no longer warns that hall-pass destinations fail, the teacher dashboard guide no longer instructs teachers to use a retired recovery banner, and Store Redemption Audit documentation now describes retained approval/rejection outcomes.

- **Hall-pass setup guide now describes saved destinations (2026-09-08)** — Removed the old “every class uses five built-ins” text and documented that the persisted destination list drives the student menu and approval queue.

- **Retired-runtime regression checks now target the canonical surfaces (2026-09-07)** — The migration idempotency test now inspects `store_products`, the versioned product table that replaced `store_items`, and stale empty analytics namespace artifacts were removed so the V1 retirement guard reflects the importable runtime.

- **Student roster exports now resolve active insurance policy names (2026-09-07)** — The `Insurance Plan` column is derived from class-scoped `INSURANCE` entitlement history and policy definitions, excluding only expired or revoked coverage, instead of being hard-coded to `None`.

- **Student detail now resolves active insurance status (2026-09-07)** — The teacher-facing detail page derives the current policy from class-scoped insurance entitlement history instead of always rendering an empty legacy placeholder.

- **Payroll guides now describe the live scheduler (2026-09-07)** — The runtime has an automatic due-cycle job, so the guides no longer tell teachers that scheduled payroll never starts; they now distinguish scheduled execution from the manual **Run Payroll** action.

- **Teacher dashboard approval queues now match their guide (2026-09-07)** — Pending insurance claims are counted and displayed from class-scoped `InsuranceClaim` rows, pending redemptions are sourced from `PendingAction` rather than all historical grants, and the dashboard guide no longer promises retired setup banners.

- **Teacher insurance claim review now uses the v2 claim lifecycle (2026-09-07)** — Claim lookup is class-scoped and UUID-based, review data is derived from `InsuranceClaim.claim_basis`, and approve/reject actions call `resolve_insurance_claim` instead of deleted v1 helper functions.

- **Several documented surface mismatches now match runtime (2026-09-07)** — Store redemption details are shown in the teacher queue, the issue-form counter reflects its 500-character validator, rent captions use the configured frequency, and Student Management help says **Delete**, matching the actual control.

- **Saving payroll settings now arms automatic payroll (2026-09-08)** — The first saved policy initializes `next_payroll_date` from the configured first payday or save time; later immutable policy versions preserve the existing scheduled occurrence so edits do not disable or reschedule the active cursor.

- **Removed the obsolete Date of Birth recovery instruction (2026-09-08)** — The student-detail setup card now reflects the live name-and-join-code recovery flow.
